Name of fill value attribute. If you wish a variable to use a different value than the above defaults, create an attribute with the same type as the variable and this reserved name. \doxylink{requirements_The}{The} value you give the attribute will be used as the fill value for that variable. \Hypertarget{netcdf_8h_a642c2ebcdafb48642490d3a498b81d4c}\index{netcdf.h@{netcdf.h}!EXTERNL@{EXTERNL}}

In HDF5 files you can set storage for each variable to be either contiguous or chunked, with nc\+\_\+def\+\_\+var\+\_\+chunking(). This define is used there. Unknown storage is used for further extensions of HDF5 storage models, which should be handled transparently by netcdf \Hypertarget{netcdf_8h_ab9a31832f7e9398dde93fb679e00b8a2}\index{netcdf.h@{netcdf.h}!NC\_CLASSIC\_MODEL@{NC\_CLASSIC\_MODEL}}

{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{netcdf_8h_a2a13ed5d0a99112c3ff372f8d7b3f32e} 
\+\#define NC\+\_\+\+FORMATX\+\_\+\+NC3~(1)}

Extended format specifier returned by nc\+\_\+inq\+\_\+format\+\_\+extended() Added in version 4.\+3.\+1. This returns the true format of the underlying data. \doxylink{requirements_The}{The} function returns two values
\begin{DoxyEnumerate}
\item a small integer indicating the underlying source type of the data. Note that this may differ from what the user sees from nc\+\_\+inq\+\_\+format() because this latter function returns what the user can expect to see thru the API.
\item A mode value indicating what mode flags are effectively set for this dataset. This usually will be a superset of the mode flags used as the argument to nc\+\_\+open or nc\+\_\+create. More or less, the \#1 values track the set of dispatch tables. \doxylink{requirements_The}{The} \#1 values are as follows. Note that CDF-\/5 returns NC\+\_\+\+FORMAT\+\_\+\+NC3, but sets the mode flag properly. 
\end{DoxyEnumerate}\Hypertarget{netcdf_8h_aff4a616129a098277a3b27c09b9a6463}\index{netcdf.h@{netcdf.h}!NC\_FORMATX\_NC4@{NC\_FORMATX\_NC4}}

\Hypertarget{netcdf_8h_a6ab02cf0827fd374085aae1deecdd97f}\index{netcdf.h@{netcdf.h}!NC\_MAX\_BYTE@{NC\_MAX\_BYTE}}
\index{NC\_MAX\_BYTE@{NC\_MAX\_BYTE}!netcdf.h@{netcdf.h}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{NC\_MAX\_BYTE}{NC\_MAX\_BYTE}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{netcdf_8h_a6ab02cf0827fd374085aae1deecdd97f} 
\+\#define NC\+\_\+\+MAX\+\_\+\+BYTE~127}

Max or min values for a type. Nothing greater/\+smaller can be stored in a net\+CDF file for their associated types. Recall that a C compiler may define int to be any length it wants, but a NC\+\_\+\+INT is {\itshape always} a 4 byte signed int. On a platform with 64 bit ints, there will be many ints which are outside the range supported by NC\+\_\+\+INT. But since NC\+\_\+\+INT is an external format, it has to mean the same thing everywhere. \Hypertarget{netcdf_8h_a321d85d78e60194a86f00b76fd2347d1}\index{netcdf.h@{netcdf.h}!NC\_MAX\_DEFLATE\_LEVEL@{NC\_MAX\_DEFLATE\_LEVEL}}
\index{NC\_MAX\_DEFLATE\_LEVEL@{NC\_MAX\_DEFLATE\_LEVEL}!netcdf.h@{netcdf.h}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{NC\_MAX\_DEFLATE\_LEVEL}{NC\_MAX\_DEFLATE\_LEVEL}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{netcdf_8h_a321d85d78e60194a86f00b76fd2347d1} 
\+\#define NC\+\_\+\+MAX\+\_\+\+DEFLATE\+\_\+\+LEVEL~9}

Maximum deflate level. \Hypertarget{netcdf_8h_a08eaafaeacac28253ac257b6d3e347cc}\index{netcdf.h@{netcdf.h}!NC\_MAX\_DIMS@{NC\_MAX\_DIMS}}
\index{NC\_MAX\_DIMS@{NC\_MAX\_DIMS}!netcdf.h@{netcdf.h}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{NC\_MAX\_DIMS}{NC\_MAX\_DIMS}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{netcdf_8h_a08eaafaeacac28253ac257b6d3e347cc} 
\+\#define NC\+\_\+\+MAX\+\_\+\+DIMS~1024 /\+\texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} not enforced after 4.\+5.\+0 \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}/\+}

Maximum for classic library.

In the classic net\+CDF model there are maximum values for the number of dimensions in the file (\doxylink{netcdf_8h_a08eaafaeacac28253ac257b6d3e347cc}{NC\+\_\+\+MAX\+\_\+\+DIMS}), the number of global or per variable attributes (NC\+\_\+\+MAX\+\_\+\+ATTRS), the number of variables in the file (NC\+\_\+\+MAX\+\_\+\+VARS), and the length of a name (NC\+\_\+\+MAX\+\_\+\+NAME).

These maximums are enforced by the interface, to facilitate writing applications and utilities. However, nothing is statically allocated to these sizes internally.

These maximums are not used for net\+CDF-\/4/\+\+HDF5 files unless they were created with the \doxylink{netcdf_8h_ab9a31832f7e9398dde93fb679e00b8a2}{NC\+\_\+\+CLASSIC\+\_\+\+MODEL} flag.

As a rule, NC\+\_\+\+MAX\+\_\+\+VAR\+\_\+\+DIMS \texorpdfstring{$<$}{<}= NC\+\_\+\+MAX\+\_\+\+DIMS.

NOTE:\+ \doxylink{requirements_The}{The} NC\+\_\+\+MAX\+\_\+\+DIMS, NC\+\_\+\+MAX\+\_\+\+ATTRS, and NC\+\_\+\+MAX\+\_\+\+VARS limits are {\itshape not} enforced after version 4.\+5.\+0 \Hypertarget{netcdf_8h_af9d98edb32fe7feb7bf9b45480812b05}\index{netcdf.h@{netcdf.h}!NC\_MAX\_HDF4\_NAME@{NC\_MAX\_HDF4\_NAME}}

\doxylink{requirements_The}{The} max size of an SD dataset name in HDF4 (from HDF4 documentation) is 64. But in in the wild we have encountered longer names. Control the HDF5 shuffle filter. In HDF5 files you can specify that a shuffle filter should be used on each chunk of a variable to improve compression for that variable. This per-\/variable shuffle property can be set with the function nc\+\_\+def\+\_\+var\+\_\+deflate(). \Hypertarget{netcdf_8h_a5e8f887b1897490757e6471e2606f062}\index{netcdf.h@{netcdf.h}!NC\_NOWRITE@{NC\_NOWRITE}}

\doxysubsection{Function Documentation}
\Hypertarget{netcdf_8h_a707a98095bbf1be1e975b1fe25e7b4ae}\index{netcdf.h@{netcdf.h}!nc\_copy\_data@{nc\_copy\_data}}
\index{nc\_copy\_data@{nc\_copy\_data}!netcdf.h@{netcdf.h}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{nc\_copy\_data()}{nc\_copy\_data()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{netcdf_8h_a707a98095bbf1be1e975b1fe25e7b4ae} 
\doxymbox{\hyperlink{netcdf_8h_a642c2ebcdafb48642490d3a498b81d4c}{EXTERNL}} int nc\+\_\+copy\+\_\+data (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{int}]{ncid}{, }\item[{\doxymbox{\hyperlink{netcdf_8h_afe40bef4fdf46f2820e9cd64c64a11b5}{nc\+\_\+type}}}]{xtypeid}{, }\item[{const void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{memory}{, }\item[{size\+\_\+t}]{count}{, }\item[{void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{copy}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

Copy vector of arbitrary type instances. This recursively walks the top-\/level instances to copy any nested data such as vlen or strings or such.

Assumes it is passed a pointer to count instances of xtype. WARNING:\+ nc\+\_\+copy\+\_\+data does not copy the top-\/level memory, but assumes a block of proper size was passed in. However nc\+\_\+copy\+\_\+data\+\_\+all does allocate top-\/level memory copy.

Should work for any netcdf format. \Hypertarget{netcdf_8h_aab9bcfb4758a7b60b55c58b2c0b0e43a}\index{netcdf.h@{netcdf.h}!nc\_reclaim\_data@{nc\_reclaim\_data}}
\index{nc\_reclaim\_data@{nc\_reclaim\_data}!netcdf.h@{netcdf.h}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{nc\_reclaim\_data()}{nc\_reclaim\_data()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{netcdf_8h_aab9bcfb4758a7b60b55c58b2c0b0e43a} 
\doxymbox{\hyperlink{netcdf_8h_a642c2ebcdafb48642490d3a498b81d4c}{EXTERNL}} int nc\+\_\+reclaim\+\_\+data (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{int}]{ncid}{, }\item[{\doxymbox{\hyperlink{netcdf_8h_afe40bef4fdf46f2820e9cd64c64a11b5}{nc\+\_\+type}}}]{xtypeid}{, }\item[{void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{memory}{, }\item[{size\+\_\+t}]{count}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

Reclaim a vector of instances of arbitrary type. Intended for use with e.\+g. nc\+\_\+get\+\_\+vara or the input to e.\+g. nc\+\_\+put\+\_\+vara. This recursively walks the top-\/level instances to reclaim any nested data such as vlen or strings or such.

Assumes it is passed a pointer to count instances of xtype. Reclaims any nested data.

WARNING:\+ nc\+\_\+reclaim\+\_\+data does not reclaim the top-\/level memory because we do not know how it was allocated. However nc\+\_\+reclaim\+\_\+data\+\_\+all does reclaim top-\/level memory.

WARNING:\+ all data blocks below the top-\/level (e.\+g. string instances) will be reclaimed, so do not call if there is any static data in the instance.

Should work for any netcdf format.
